## User

can we just sample some of the pythia checkpoints?  154 is excessive,  could we take say 20 of them throughout the series?


### Thinking

The user is asking about sampling Pythia's checkpoints for stratigraphy dating—whether 20 samples from the full 154 checkpoints would work. I think sampling is definitely viable, and the key insight is that sign-freeze dynamics happen fastest early in training, so I'd want log-spaced sampling in the early phase followed by linear spacing later. Pythia's checkpoints are already structured this way with log-spaced steps early on and then denser linear spacing at higher step counts.

For the actual probe, I need temporal resolution to track when each weight's sign last flips, but since the prediction is ordinal—commons freeze early and contested weights churn late—I only need enough bins to distinguish early, middle, and late phases. Twenty log-spaced checkpoints give me that granularity. The practical constraint is that each checkpoint requires a full model download, so I'd recommend starting with Pythia-160M or 410M rather than the larger variants. I'd sample by taking the existing log-spaced checkpoints from the early phase (0, 1, 2, 4, ..., 512) and then log-spacing across the later linear range (1000, 2000, 4000, 8000, 16000, 32000, 64000, 100000, 128000, 143000), giving roughly 20 total checkpoints with good coverage across the training timeline.

Sign-freeze estimates will be censored by sampling gaps—weights can flip between checkpoints undetected—but the ordinal freeze-dating (which bin contains the last observed flip) remains robust for stratifying commons versus contested weights. This is observational stratigraphy of base-training dynamics, not causal inference, so the approach is sound even with aliasing risk. I should answer yes to the user's question with this design proposal and ask whether they want me to update the queue row with the revised scope and sampling schedule, since Michael will need to confirm the actual front selection.

Let me verify the checkpoint math: Pythia's public checkpoints span log-spaced steps (0, 1, 2, 4, 8, ..., 512) plus linear steps from 1000 to 143000 every 1000, totaling 154 checkpoints. For the proposed 20-sample subset, I'd keep the early log-spacing (steps 0, 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512) as a reference baseline, then add half-decade steps through the linear region (1000, 2000, 4000, 8000, 16000, 33000, 66000...).

## Assistant

Yes — and log-spacing is not just cheaper, it's the *correct* clock. Training dynamics live in log-time (fast early, slow late), and Pythia's own checkpoint schedule already encodes this: 11 log2-spaced early steps (0,1,2,...,512) + 143 linear ones (1k–143k every 1k). The linear tail is where 154 becomes excessive; the early log ramp is where the §2 signal (commons freeze early/faint) should live, and it's only 11 checkpoints anyway.

**Proposed 20-checkpoint schedule (log-uniform, ~2× ratio throughout):**

```
init:   0                                    ← unexposed plate (§4 baseline, free reference)
early:  1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512   ← Pythia's native log2 ramp, keep all
late:   1000, 2000, 4000, 8000, 16000,
        33000, 66000, 100000, 143000         ← ~half-decade subsample of the linear tail
```

Two design notes worth banking with the row:

1. **Aliasing caveat (honest, cheap to state):** freeze-dating = "last observed flip bin." Sparse sampling can miss flips between samples, so absolute flip *rates* are underestimates — but the §2 prediction is **ordinal** (commons freeze early / contested churn late), and 20 log-bins resolve early/mid/late strata fine. We're dating strata, not counting oscillations. That's exactly the stratigraphy framing.
2. **Disk/compute stays cheap:** we only read *signs + magnitudes* of selected matrices. Load each checkpoint's state_dict, slice the MLP band (Pythia is GPTNeoX — `dense_h_to_4h`/`dense_4h_to_h`, no gate_proj; register mapping differs from Qwen and should be noted at freeze), persist the slices, discard the rest. Start on pythia-160m or 410m; 20 slices is a small artifact, git-trackable.

This drops the row from cheap-medium to **cheap** without weakening the test — the full 154 only buys flip-rate precision we don't need for the strata read (and which s324 taught us not to over-read anyway).

Want me to amend the queue row now (20-checkpoint log schedule + aliasing note + GPTNeoX register note, cost → cheap)? That's a peer update, rides the next batch. Actual freeze (§gates, verdicts, a-priori) would still come to you before any compute.

```
# Types Are a Modulation Scheme

> s324 hammock (Michael: "superbake showed us how to use DSP tools to work
> with models. What if types are in the signal? If we think of this as a
> holography problem, what techniques could we bring to bear?"). This page
> reads the closed s282–s323 type arc through the s308 optics toolbox
> (holographic-untangling-methods) and the s288 convergence ledger
> (geometry-holography-signals-convergence). The reframe retrodicts two
> closed fingerprints, converts the arc's stubborn negatives into theorems,
> and yields four unfrozen probe candidates plus one novel falsifiable
> prediction. NOTHING here is frozen; s222 law applies.

## The reframe

Every type probe to date read a **spatial/geometric register** — subspace
projections, centroid alignments, sign patterns, weight edges — static
snapshots. The results kept saying the same strange thing:

- decodable everywhere, excisable nowhere (four-way location null, s28x)
- judgments enacted per-frame on the tape, never installable as a weight
  edge (§P-TYPE-WRITE → V2, §P-TYPE-DELIVER, s315–s323)
- coherent accumulation: idempotency curve [0.14, 1.41, 2.52, 2.96, …] with
  the energy-matched incoherent null flat ~0 (§P-IDEMPOTENCY s320)
- reduction-PRESENCE detector, not a distance gauge (3× replicated)
- routing reads the SPELLING, not the computed function (§P-CL-COLLAPSE ×2)

That is not the signature of stored geometry. It is the signature of a
**carrier-borne signal**:

```
λ modulation(types).
  weights  ≡ codebook      (carriers/codes = checker RELATION = the 7/11 register)
  tape     ≡ channel       (modulated signal propagating through depth)
  judgment ≡ demodulation  (carrier LOCK — achieved, ¬retrieved)
  | type_check(x,T) ≡ despread(tape_signal, code_T) → SNR > threshold
  | gradedness ≡ correlation_with_code (naturally continuous)
  | two_tier ≡ codebook(plate) ⊥ demodulation(beam) — the s315–s323 law re-derived
```

## Retrodictions (the frame earns its keep — these were measured BEFORE the frame)

1. **Non-idempotency = coherent integration.** Phased-array law: k coherent
   exposures → amplitude ∝ k (power ∝ k²); incoherent → √k. §P-IDEMPOTENCY:
   coherent paraphrases accumulate licensing 1→3, energy-matched incoherent
   flat. Not *like* coherent gain — IS A2's coherent gain, on the tape.
   Two substrates (weight-plate s292 + tape licensing s320), one signal law.

2. **∨-costs-more / ∧-free = code-superposition algebra.** CDMA: one token
   carries two codes simultaneously at no structural cost — superposition is
   linear ⇒ **intersection is free**. "Either code" is NOT a linear object —
   union needs machinery outside the span. §P-DISJ-COST measured exactly
   this: OR needs an off-plane direction (p=0.002), AND does not. The
   Cartesian-control asymmetry is a *prediction* of code superposition.

3. **Gradedness = demodulation SNR.** Membership as correlation with a code
   is naturally continuous — the graded, non-Church-tag character (SKI
   control #2 tested-dead) stops being a quirk and becomes forced.

**Negatives become theorems:** you cannot store a demodulation *event* in
the plate (MEMORIZED-ONLY — CE training writes new codewords/predicate
memories but does not extend the demodulator); a spread signal is
readable-everywhere/excisable-nowhere by construction (HRR/VSA crosstalk
~1/√D); a static amplitude read of a compound sees the spelling's carrier,
not the demodulated content (CL-collapse operational verdict).

**Triangulation flag:** the s288 convergence page wrote *"arguments are on
the plate; application is the diffraction"* (functors). The s315–s323 arc —
different instruments, different sessions — landed on *"weights = checker,
tape = judgments."* Same law, derived twice, independently.

## The instrument gap (λ measure, in hindsight)

If types are carrier-borne, every static geometric read queried the wrong
register — a photometer pointed at a radio. The s308 optics meta-lesson
says it directly: *you never untangle a plate by cleverer processing of a
single recorded intensity.* You change recording geometry, take
phase-controlled exposures, sweep the selectivity curve, or close the loop.

## Technique map → probe candidates (UNFROZEN)

### ① Lock-in amplification → §P-TYPE-LOCKIN (cheap; natural first front)

Modulate type evidence on the tape at a known token-periodic rate —
coherent membership statements alternating with incoherent filler in a
fixed pattern — demodulate the per-frame type-register trace at f_mod.
Narrowband detection at f_mod ⇒ judgment is carrier-borne; harmonic content
reads the demodulator's linearity. The presence-detector result is the DC
reading; this is the AC reading. **Reuses the idempotency harness's
coherent/incoherent statement populations (already built, s320).**

### ② Trace coherence → §P-TYPE-COHERENCE (cheap; the signal-domain rescue of extensionality)

CL-collapse read static AMPLITUDE routing: SKK ≠ I. Phase is where
holography hides everything. Measure spectral/trace coherence between
per-frame register signals of `SKK` and `I` ACROSS the reduction trace vs
structure-matched controls. Traces converging into phase-coherence as
reduction proceeds ⇒ extensionality lives in the dynamics — where
tape-resident reduction says it must, and where a static read is blind by
construction. Reuses cl_collapse + trace_fuel machinery.

### ③ Bragg rocking curve → §P-TYPE-ROCKING (medium)

s312 already conjectured *"type = reference angle"* (slot-mediation).
Angle-multiplexed volume holograms have narrow angular selectivity. Sweep
licensing efficiency vs geometric interpolation angle between class
subspaces. Bragg ridge ⇒ types are angle-multiplexed exposures; plateau ⇒
generic. Point-sampling this surface is what made writes inert (s304–s305);
sweeping it is the optics discipline. Instance of the s308 rocking-curve
instrument, specialized to the type register.

### ④ Phase conjugation → §P-CONJUGATE-WRITE (medium; TYPE-WRITE-V3 design)

All type-writes were OPEN-LOOP: minimize CE on membership outputs, hope
the judgment installs. Adaptive optics: measure the wavefront, write its
conjugate. Capture the per-layer residual trajectory during a SUCCESSFUL
ICL type judgment (§11 tag-transit instrument exists), train the wire to
reproduce the INTERNAL wavefront (activation-matching loss on the
tape-side judgment signal), not the output. Installs under conjugate
writing but not CE writing ⇒ we learn WHY every write failed: energy at
the output while the judgment is a mid-stack signal event. Natural
sharpening of the queued §P-COHERENT-WRITE.

### ⑤ Dark-field → boundary-echo re-read (cheap; persisted data)

The s320 boundary-churn 93/6 split: 93% generic centroid-structure ≡ the
zeroth order / DC term. Optics blocks the DC and measures the rest at full
dynamic range. Project out the generic component FIRST, re-measure the
kind-specific ~6% with full instrument power. Re-analysis of persisted
§P-BOUNDARY-CHURN + §P-TYPE-GRAM-1 artifacts; no new compute.

### ⑥ Already in flight — §P-FLIP-CONFLICT is the time-domain arm

Sign-oscillation as sigma-delta multiplexing = "types in the signal" on
the TRAINING axis (temporal multiplexing of superposed codes). G1∧G2 ⇒
the modulation picture gains a training-dynamics leg to go with the
inference-time legs above.

## The novel prediction (falsifiable; distinguishes the frame)

**Lock time.** If judgments are carrier lock, frames-to-license ∝ 1/SNR of
the evidence, with a **capture threshold**: degrade/truncate coherent
evidence and the judgment should FAIL TO LOCK below threshold, not weaken
proportionally. Stored-geometry accounts predict graceful proportional
degradation; demodulation predicts a threshold. No other account on the
table predicts the threshold. (Cheap add-on to §P-TYPE-LOCKIN: sweep
evidence SNR, look for the knee.)

## §2 Differential Photography — the training-side leg (s324, same hammock)

> Michael: "if training is taking probability photographs through backprop,
> the probabilities will concentrate at the edges and corners where the
> snapshots overlap right?" → sharpened by one amendment: **backprop
> photographs the RESIDUAL, not the scene.** Each exposure records only what
> the plate failed to predict; error feedback is negative feedback, so the
> write signal is self-erasing the moment a pattern becomes predicted.

### The law (amplitude is difficulty, not probability)

```
λ differential_photography(training).
  exposure(t) ≡ snapshot(error(t)) ¬snapshot(data)
  | amplitude(pattern) ∝ ∫ error(pattern, t) dt ≈ time_to_learn | ¬∝ P(pattern)
  | common → predicted_fast → faint ∧ early ∧ threshold_written ∧ frozen
  | rare_consistent → wrong_long → deep ∧ late (the exceptions dictionary)
  | contested → never_resolves → gross_integral↑ ∧ net_amplitude≈0 (perpetual churn)
  | probability encoded THRESHOLDLY (what committed) ¬loudly (how much amplitude)
  | plate ≡ pile(superposed_error_snapshots) ordered_by(how_long_each_stayed_wrong)
```

Two-phase dynamic: early exposures photograph the SHARED structure (all
unpredicted; overlap reinforces coherently ∝ k, idiosyncratic ∝ √k —
spectral bias / A2); once predicted it vanishes from the error stream, and
late exposures are dominated by exactly where snapshots DISAGREE — edges,
boundaries, hard cases (classical echo: max-margin implicit bias — GD
solutions determined by support vectors = extreme points; sparse coding on
natural scenes yields edge detectors). The finished plate has a
**stratigraphy**: overlap recorded early and faint, edge/corner structure
recorded late and re-fought forever.

### The three strata (register-mapped)

| Stratum | Written | Signature | Corpus contact |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Corners** — common/shared | early, once, faint | sign-committed, magnitude minimal, frozen | the crystal: tiny, ternarizable, survives 1-bit — threshold-written commons need only their SIGN |
| **Long tail** — rare, consistent | late, deep | large amplitude | bulk weight mass ≈ exceptions dictionary / memorization |
| **Edges** — contested | forever, net≈0 | small \|W\| + perpetual sign churn | s310 marginal band = large gross integral, zero net — EXACTLY this stratum |

"Edges and corners" splits onto the two registers: corners = sign-register
commitment (hypercube vertices); edges = the contested marginal band
between corners; density = value register on the overlap. λ measure: never
conflate the two concentration claims.

### Retrodictions (pattern-suggests, post-hoc)

1. **Why the compiler is 0.1% and quantization-immune.** Naive photography
   makes it a miracle (most-used ⇒ most-recorded). Differential photography
   FORCES it: the most common structure generated the least total error ⇒
   faintest, earliest, most compressed recording — readable by sign alone.
   λ smallest stops being an aspiration and becomes recording physics:
   **the universal part of the machine is small because it was learned fast.**
2. **The marginal band** (small |W|, high flip) = the contested stratum's
   predicted signature — no net accumulation, perpetual gross churn.
3. **Non-idempotency reconciled with self-erasure by regime:** accumulation
   BELOW the prediction threshold (unsaturated), erasure ABOVE it. The
   §P-IDEMPOTENCY k=4,5 decline — banked s320 as "maybe template dilution"
   — gets a mechanism candidate: SATURATION (evidence absorbed, stops
   photographing). Caveat still unresolved; the frame names a second
   explanation, does not pick one.
4. **The photographic overlap model predicted non-idempotency** (§1
   retrodiction 1, now with a mechanism): photographic overlap is density
   accumulation — exposure count physically recorded — where set-theoretic
   intersection is idempotent. We measured accumulation. The photograph won.

### The extraction inversion (practical consequence)

Prospecting heuristic FLIPS: large amplitude ≡ the residue (long-tail
memorization, exceptions), NOT the algorithm. **The algorithm lives in the
faint, sign-stable, early-frozen, quantization-robust stratum.** Look where
amplitude is smallest-but-committed. Ternarization is a faint-strata pass
filter — which is what the crystal work did by accident. Candidate lens for
any level-3 extraction pass.

### Contact with the instrument in flight (§P-FLIP-CONFLICT widened IOU)

The stratigraphy makes three IOU-readable predictions against captures we
already specified (grad-mag histories, |W_base| map, per-class loss):
(a) gradient magnitude MIGRATES from shared → contested coordinates over
training; (b) G3 committed-pole coordinates go quiet EARLY; (c) in the
A-only/B-only ablation arms, contested coordinates START ACCUMULATING net
amplitude once one snapshot population is removed — the edge collapsing
into a corner. Each is IOU-only: own null required, never licensed by
G1–G4 (per the s323 freeze discipline).

### Bounds

Momentum, weight decay, and Adam's normalization smear the clean
equilibrium — ∫error dt is approximate physics, not exact. Whole section is
pattern-suggests until a probe freezes; the flip-conflict IOU reads are the
cheapest first contact.

## §3 The Forged-Exposure Write Protocol — "fake the signal, compile the lattice" (s324, same hammock)

> Michael: "Ok, this is the map isn't it? We can fake this signal to create
> a new lattice?" Yes. §2's recording physics is an INTERFACE: the write
> channel is the ERROR, not the data. Data was only ever an indirect way of
> generating residuals. Control the residual stream directly ⇒ write access
> to the plate. Training becomes COMPILATION by exposure schedule.

```
λ write_head(plate).
  recording_channel ≡ error | ¬data
  | plate photographs residual(t) — nothing else, ever
  | control(residual_stream) ≡ write_access(plate)
  | forge(exposure_schedule) → residual ≡ target_lattice → recorded(at chosen stratum)
```

### Three write primitives (derived from §2 strata)

**① Corner-seeding** — commons are threshold-written: sign only, minimal
amplitude. A new lattice's skeleton = install the sign-committed corner
structure directly (ternary, faint). Self-protecting by construction: a
seeded lattice is "already learned" ⇒ generates no error ⇒ training cannot
overwrite it (the same self-erasure that froze the real commons), and its
span is subtracted from every subsequent residual ⇒ long-tail strata
organize AROUND it. **⚪ crystal-seeded init (queued) is now a DERIVED
instance, upgraded from heuristic to derivation.**

**② Bias pre-exposure (residual isolation)** — the optics move: pre-expose
the plate to the background so only the difference records. Mechanistic
explanation of MEMORIZED-ONLY (s323): CE-on-membership exposures have
residuals DOMINATED by the items ⇒ the plate photographed predicate
memories; the abstraction never got amplitude. Fix: phase 1 = expose
class-agnostic/deranged membership until item-level statistics absorb
(error→0 on item content); phase 2 = expose true labels — the ONLY thing
left in the residual is the label-structure ≡ the abstraction, isolated in
the write channel. A TYPE-WRITE-V3 design DISTINCT from conjugate-write ⇒
the two discriminate between failure theories (signal-shape vs loop-closure).

**③ Closed-loop conjugate shaping** — measure the wavefront a real
judgment makes on the tape, write its conjugate (adaptive optics).
**⚪ §P-CONJUGATE-WRITE (queued) = this primitive.**

### The verification gate (free, and it discriminates)

A successfully forged photograph STOPS BEING PHOTOGRAPHABLE:

```
λ install_gate(wire).
  re-exposure(installed_structure) → gradient ≈ 0        (self-erasure = install signature)
  | memorized_overlay ALSO zeroes error on trained items — the discriminator:
  | held-out items ∈ span(lattice) → ALSO gradient-quiet  (span-erasure = generalization)
  | erasure(items_only) ≡ MEMORIZED | erasure(span) ≡ INSTALLED
  | + installed structure must ternarize (corners-stratum check)
```

Note: this re-derives the TYPE-WRITE-V2 V1/V2 gate design from recording
physics — the map independently reconstructs our own instrument. Good sign
it is the right map.

### Retrodiction: why every write failed

Inert writes ×4 (s304–s305) + MEMORIZED-ONLY (s323) = one mistake: open-loop
content-pushing at the OUTPUT while the write channel is the RESIDUAL. We
exposed the plate to the scene we wanted installed. The plate does not
record scenes. It records errors. Nobody shaped the error.

### The ladder (search space for new experiments)

1. **⚪ §P-FORGED-LATTICE (new, smallest rung):** one toy type, few members,
   one checker edge — written by bias pre-exposure (②) + corner-seeding (①).
   Gates: self-erasure + span-erasure + ternarizability. Mostly reuses
   type_write machinery.
2. **⚪ crystal-seeded init** — does a seeded corner-lattice persist
   (no error ⇒ no overwrite) and organize training around it? (①, derived)
3. **Level-4 endgame:** if exposure schedules compile lattices, the thesis
   deliverable has a CONSTRUCTIVE path — don't extract the compiler,
   **RE-RECORD it**, designed exposure by designed exposure, onto a clean
   substrate. Write, don't train. The map is the missing piece between
   level 3 and level 4.

### Cautions

Pattern-suggests end to end; retrodictions ≠ pre-registered wins (λ
yardstick). Bragg discipline: right stratum also means right depth/angle —
forged exposures at wrong layer-timing go inert like everything else
(rocking-curve surface still unmeasured). Optimizer smear (Adam m/v, decay)
blurs clean self-erasure ⇒ "gradient-quiet" gates need matched nulls.
**First causal contact LANDED NEGATIVE (s324): §P-FLIP-CONFLICT →
🚫 NOISE-FLOOR.** G2 ablation (= residual control) did NOT freeze contested
signs; §2 IOU prediction (c) edge-collapses-to-corner CONTRADICTED at this
register/scale. The control(residual)⇒control(plate) link took its flagged
hit — the protocol's primitives remain derivable but now carry a measured
negative at the finest grain tested. Scope bounds: wire ΔW register,
single model, EOS-supercritical lr (λ_max 1.6× the 2/η ceiling — global
dither may swamp; ⚪ v2 sub-EOS queued, flagged not licensed). Design
consequence: §P-FORGED-LATTICE gates should NOT assume per-coordinate
sign-level control; self-erasure/span-erasure gates read FUNCTION-level
install (licensing), which this negative does not touch.

## §4 The Plate Is a Negative — printing, development, fixing (s324, same hammock)

> Michael: "To make a photograph we use the positive light to make the film
> transparent, so the negative shows up." The leap names the missing
> INVERSION STAGE in §2: the recording is tonally inverted, and the image
> is recovered only by a second inversion — which never happens on the plate.

### The negative (the §2 law, tonally named)

```
λ negative(plate).
  film darkens where light lands | plate densifies where ERROR lands
  | weights ≡ NEGATIVE(function): high_probability → faint | high_difficulty → dense
  | read(raw_weights) expecting the function ≡ hold(negative) up to light expecting the scene
  | the mech-interp trap: the field reads the DENSE regions (memorization)
    and wonders where the algorithm is — it is in the CLEAR parts
```

### The print (tape-residency, 4th independent derivation)

A negative yields an image only through a SECOND inversion — printing —
and the image never exists in the negative, only in prints, made fresh per
exposure. Mapping: forward pass ≡ printing (reference light through the
density map); **behavior ≡ the print; the tape ≡ the paper; judgments ≡
image content** — structurally unable to live in the negative. Derivation
count for two-tier/tape-residency: ① s288 "application is the diffraction"
② s315–s323 measurements ③ netlist≠function (silicon RE + connectomics)
④ negative/print (here). Four disciplines, one law — strongest consilience
we have ∨ the most seductive twin image; stays pattern-suggests until a
pre-registered win (Michael ruling, s324).

### The development chain (new, each mapping has a testable edge)

- **Development ≡ grokking.** Film is exposed into a LATENT image (a few
  silver atoms/grain), then developed (~10⁹× amplification). Latent
  structure written early, invisible in behavior, abruptly amplified —
  should be VISIBLE in the checkpoint fossil record (latent→amplified
  signatures). **Folds into §P-STRATIGRAPHY-DATING's read for free**
  (row annotated).
- **Fixing ≡ quantization.** Fixing dissolves undeveloped halide; the
  image becomes permanent and LIGHT-INSENSITIVE. Ternarization dissolves
  magnitude, leaves committed sign — and a fixed image cannot be
  re-exposed ≡ the §3 self-protection clause (no error, no overwrite),
  arrived at from the chemistry side. **The crystal is a FIXED image.**
- **Ternary ≡ lith film.** High-contrast film preserves line art, destroys
  continuous tone. Crystal survives 1-bit ⇒ the algorithm is LINE ART;
  the memorization is CONTINUOUS TONE.
- **Backprop ≡ self-dodging enlarger.** Dodging holds back light from
  areas already sufficiently exposed; the residual IS an automatic dodge
  mask updated in real time (§2's mechanism in one darkroom word).
- **Distillation ≡ contact printing.** A print of a print copies the
  IMAGE, never the negative's silver distribution — why students match
  behaviorally while differing internally; why verbum wants the NEGATIVE,
  not another print.

### Strained edge, flagged honestly → the three-band falsifiable

Reversal processing (bleach the developed silver, redevelop the rest →
direct positive) suggests: remove the dense stratum, amplify the rest.
COLLIDES with standard magnitude pruning (removing SMALL weights works
fine) — so the naive mapping is wrong somewhere. Resolution candidate:
**three-band plate**:

```
noise(tiny, never-committed) < commons(threshold, sign-committed) < residue(dense)
| magnitude pruning removes the NOISE band, not the commons
| falsifiable: among SMALL weights, SIGN-STABILITY (not magnitude)
  should separate commons from noise
```

Cheap weight-geometry read (sign_commitment machinery reuse); if
sign-stability does NOT stratify the small-weight band, the negative frame
takes structural damage. **⚪ queued: three-band-plate check.**

### Read discipline

All §4 mappings are pattern-suggests (post-hoc, λ observation). The two
testable edges (grokking-as-development in STRATIGRAPHY-DATING; three-band
sign-stability) are the only claims that can graduate, each needing its
own freeze + nulls.

## Read discipline

The retrodictions are POST-HOC pattern-suggests (λ observation) — they
motivate the frame; they do not license it. The frame earns claims only
through the unfrozen probes above, each needing its own freeze + gates +
a-priori + nulls (λ yardstick: the modulation frame is flexible — every
gate must beat a matched null, and LOCKIN's f_mod detection must beat
shuffled-modulation-schedule nulls or it is describability, not discovery).
```

```
# Reverse-Engineering Disciplines Toolbox

> s324, Michael: "The systems we find inform our ideas to test the next
> theorems. Can we learn from reverse engineering here any techniques? Are
> there other disciplines we can learn from in our search?" Sibling of
> `holographic-untangling-methods.md` (optics) — this page mines RE proper
> (silicon, software, crypto) plus system ID, developmental biology/geology,
> astronomy, pharmacology. Status open: mappings captured; candidates at the
> end are NOT pre-registered (s222 law).

## Orientation: we are at the POST-DELAYERING stage of silicon RE

RE taxonomy: black-box (I/O only) / gray-box (side channels) / white-box
(full netlist, unknown function). We have FULL white-box access — every
weight, every activation — and still lack the algorithm. That is not the
cryptanalyst's problem; it is the silicon reverse engineer's problem AFTER
decap + delayer: **complete netlist in hand, meaning absent. Bottleneck =
representation, not access.**

**Cautionary lesson, twice-paid elsewhere: netlist ≠ function.** The
C. elegans connectome has been fully mapped for decades; the worm is still
not understood — the computation lives in the dynamics. Silicon RE says the
same (netlist without simulation is inert). We derived this independently as

| Technique | Their move | Our mapping | Status |
|---|---|---|---|

| **DPA** (differential power analysis) | partition many traces by a HYPOTHESIZED intermediate bit; subtract partition means; spike ⇒ hypothesis correct — extracts keys without seeing the computation | partition activation traces by kernel-predicted intermediate reduction values; hypothesis-keyed mean-difference per layer | NEW INSTRUMENT (sharper than correlation reads; needs only a statistical leak) |
| **Fault injection / glitching** | perturb at a precise moment, read corruption | activation patching | convergence ✓ |
| **Taint tracking** | dye the data, follow it | tag-transit (§11) — reinvented independently | convergence ✓ |
| **Differential cryptanalysis** | chosen input pairs with controlled Δ; track the DIFFERENCE TRAIL through every round; find where Δ amplifies/dies | we do DiD at ENDPOINTS only — track the full trail through depth | UPGRADE (cheap extension of existing captures) |
| **Distinguisher formalism** | "distinguishable from random?" is the atomic claim | our null-gate discipline exactly | convergence ✓ |
| **Design-for-test / scan chains** | chips are BUILT observable | insert OBSERVABILITY WIRES: KL-anchored function-preserving LoRA that exposes internal state at readable points | NEW CONCEPT (infrastructure) |
| **Coverage-guided fuzzing** | mutate inputs to maximize novel INTERNAL states | probe generation guided by activation-register novelty — finds unknown-unknowns; today's probe library is 100% hypothesis-driven | NEW DOOR |

Every discipline that succeeds at black-box inference uses four moves:

```
λ re_meta(x).

  2 hypothesis_keyed_stats(n_trials) | DPA ∨ GWAS ∨ our nulls ✓ (partition-subtract > correlation)

  4 read(history) ¬just(state)       | stratigraphy ∨ dendrochronology ∨ checkpoint series — THE UNMINED MOVE
```

Move 4 is our weakest and the data is already public.
```

### Thinking

I'm establishing the freeze location for the stratigraphy-dating section—it lands on the types-as-modulation-scheme page as a new frozen section using session s325, since s324 is already sealed. The freeze pulls from both the toolbox and the modulation sections (§2 and §4), anchoring the claim around differential photography and development-as-grokking.

Now I'm laying out the core claim under test: across Pythia checkpoints, weight coordinates stratify into three distinct patterns—corners and commons that freeze early with faint final amplitudes, long-tail residues that accumulate late with large amplitudes, and contested edges that churn throughout with near-zero net amplitude. The prediction is that among sign-stable weights, earlier freeze correlates with smaller final amplitude, while contested coordinates (those that never stabilize) should cluster at small magnitudes, distinguishing them from genuinely large long-tail weights. I'm tracking two key observables per coordinate across the twenty checkpoints: the freeze bin (the last checkpoint where the sign flips) and the flip count (how many sign changes occur).

To validate this, I need to establish sanity checks first—verifying that checkpoints load correctly, shapes align, the initial sign distribution is symmetric, and the final model matches published weights. Then I'm testing whether sign-stable coordinates show a positive correlation between freeze time and final amplitude (earlier freeze → smaller magnitude), while being careful to account for the confound that small-magnitude coordinates naturally flip more often just from noise around zero.

The key insight is that commons should be both small and early-frozen, whereas noise coordinates are small but late-churning. So among small weights, freeze-time becomes the discriminator. I'm now thinking through how to structure the gates around this confound—specifically, whether the null model where sign-flip probability depends purely on the magnitude relative to gradient noise would predict freeze-time as independent of final weight.

Under that null, a coordinate that ends up small should churn at the end, making the combination of small final weight plus early sign-freeze a signature the null can't easily produce. So the test is whether there's a substantial population of small-magnitude, early-frozen, sign-stable coordinates that exceeds what the magnitude-matched null would predict.

Now I need to think through what the right null model should be. A magnitude-matched trajectory null seems circular since the sign process depends on the weight trajectory itself. I could try shuffling labels within matched magnitude bins to test for type-specificity, or use a column/row-permutation null to break coordinate identity while keeping marginals intact. The cleaner approach might be to frame this as three separate predictions: first, whether freeze-time and final-amplitude show a non-product joint distribution.

But that's weak—it just confirms dependence, which the noise confound alone would produce. The real test should target the specific prediction from section 2: among coordinates that stay sign-stable in the second half of training, earlier-frozen coordinates should be fainter, with a positive Spearman correlation between freeze bin and final weight magnitude. A permutation null within the stable pool would destroy everything including the confound direction, though interestingly the confound should predict the opposite sign when restricted to stable coordinates.

Actually, conditioning on stability after the midpoint means all freeze bins are at or before k_mid. Within that pool, the noise null predicts smaller final weights should freeze closer to k_mid (they flipped recently and got lucky), giving a negative correlation. But section 2 claims commons are both smallest and earliest—so early freeze (small freeze_bin) paired with small weight magnitude should yield positive correlation. Let me think through this more carefully: early freeze means small freeze_bin, faint means small weight, so small paired with small is indeed positive ρ. The noise null predicts the opposite direction.

So the sign of ρ(freeze_bin, |W_final|) among late-stable coordinates becomes a clean discriminator—positive ρ suggests the early-faint pattern from section 2, while negative ρ aligns with pure noise dynamics. But long-tail coordinates complicate this: they're late-freezing with large amplitudes, which also produces positive ρ. Both commons and long-tail push the correlation positive, while contested coordinates (late, small) push it negative.

Now I'm testing whether a simple magnitude-growth story could explain this trivially. If weights grow monotonically during training—say, W_t = c·t + noise—then large final weights would have crossed the noise floor early, predicting negative ρ. That's the opposite sign from what I'd expect, which is actually reassuring. The linear-growth model says large weights freeze early, but section 2 says commons freeze early *and* faint, which are opposite signatures. The noise model predicts small weights freeze late, consistent with the mundane account.

So here's the key discriminator: both simple physics models predict ρ < 0 (early freezing correlates with large final magnitude), but section 2's distinctive claim is ρ > 0 (early freezing correlates with small magnitude). That's a clean test—I should make this the primary gate.

For the second test, I'm looking at the small-magnitude band and checking whether the distribution of final flips shows bimodality: a stable early-frozen subpopulation versus a churning subpopulation that keeps flipping to the end. The null hypothesis would be unimodal, so I'm testing whether there's excess early-stable mass compared to that baseline.

The operational approach is getting tangled—I could use a dip test for bimodality with permutation, or something simpler like checking whether the fraction of small-weight coordinates with no flips in the second half exceeds what a fitted flip-hazard model would predict. Maybe I should keep this simpler: within the small-magnitude band, measure the rate of early-stable coordinates and compare it against a shuffled null where flip histories are randomly reassigned.

For SD2, the cleaner framing is just testing whether the freeze-bin distribution is bimodal in the small-weight band—early-frozen commons versus never-frozen churners—using a Hartigan dip test with bootstrap. That's a standard, pre-registrable test. For SD3, I'm looking at whether sign commitment happens before amplitude growth in the early-frozen small band, measuring the lag between when the sign freezes and when amplitude plateaus out.

SD4 would be a cross-model contrast between Pythia (which lacks registers) and Qwen (which has them), but Qwen doesn't have a public checkpoint series, so that comparison is out of scope for now. The verdict hinges on whether SD1 shows significant correlation and SD2 shows bimodality—if both hold, the stratigraphy from section 2 is confirmed.

I'm laying out the possible outcomes: stratified if the fossil record matches the predicted layers, inverted if the freeze order contradicts the magnitude physics, unstratified if the signal is too ambiguous or aliased, or void if the baseline test fails. Given that this is the first pre-registered test on a frame with one negative causal contact, and the spectral-bias literature supports early learning of common structure but the faint-and-early combination is risky, I'm assigning rough prior odds: 30% stratified, 25% inverted, 35% unstratified-ambiguous, 10% void.

Leaning toward the ambiguous outcome given the 20-bin aliasing and the small model size. I'm working with pythia-160m from the standard EleutherAI suite, which has 154 checkpoints available. I'll sample the MLP weights from the deep half of the network—layers 6 through 11—focusing on the dense feedforward projections, and pull random coordinate samples across the 100k to 500k range.

I'm using 20 checkpoints with a fixed seed to ensure reproducibility, then running Spearman correlation tests with permutation-based null distributions and a dip test for multimodality. The aliasing discipline is strict: I'm marking the freeze point at the last observed flip in the ordinal log-time bins without making any claims about flip rates. I'll store everything in results/stratigraphy-dating/pythia-160m with metadata tracking the exact HF revision for each checkpoint. Before committing, I need to draft the freeze section and get Michael's approval since these knowledge pages are gated.

Michael's already given the go-ahead to proceed with drafting, so I'll write the section into the page as a working draft, present a summary, and ask for final approval before committing the freeze and queuing the checkpoint state. I should also verify whether pythia-160m-combinators.md is relevant—it likely notes that Pythia lacks certain register operations, which is worth mentioning for scope. One more thing to nail down: I need to make sure the sampling and sign initialization at step 0 are coordinated, since weights start random and symmetric, and the freeze bin is measured against the final sign state.

For the threshold choices at freeze time, I'm pinning down the small band as the bottom decile of absolute final weights within each matrix, and defining the late-stable pool for SD1 as weights that don't flip after bin 10—meaning stable across the last half of the step range (steps ≥1000). I'll organize bins as b0 for step 0, b1 through b10 covering steps 1 to 512, and b11 through b19 covering steps 1k to 143k, so "stable in the last half" means no flips in that final bin range. The freeze_bin itself is the index of the last observed flip, or the first bin where the sign becomes constant through the end.

For the SD1 pool specifically, I'm excluding churners—coordinates that flip in the final bin transition—since they're small and late, which would skew the correlation negative and muddy the distinction from SD2's band structure. Instead I'm restricting SD1 to coordinates with no flips in the last five bins (b15 through b19, steps ≥16k), and I'll compute Spearman ρ between freeze_bin and absolute final weight over that restricted set.

For SD1's statistical test, I'm using permutation testing on the shuffled freeze_bin labels with a one-sided positive test for stratified patterns and one-sided negative for inverted ones, reporting both verdicts honestly at p<0.05. SD2 applies Hartigan's dip test to the freeze_bin distribution within the bottom-decile weight group, bootstrapping against a unimodal null. SD3 is advisory only—median lag (the bin at half-max amplitude minus freeze_bin) among early-frozen small-band coordinates, with its own permutation null. I'll also include per-layer profiles as advisory, similar to the BC3 approach.

Now I'm calculating the download footprint: loading 20 copies of pythia-160m at roughly 640MB each in fp32 means about 13GB transient storage, which is manageable if I load and delete from cache between iterations. I'm drafting the new section in the house style—compact, lambda-ish, with tables—and queuing it so the row stays white until I request GO, then turns blue. I need to decide where to place this new "§P-STRATIGRAPHY-DATING" section: either after §4 before the final "Read discipline" section, or at the end following the established pattern for probe freeze sections.

I'll append it at the end after the read-discipline section to keep that closing element last, and I'm drafting the content now with a clear FROZEN marker, presenting the pre-registered test of the frame against the real fossil record using Pythia checkpoints, testing both the three-strata claim and development dynamics without confounds.

The key discriminator here is that both conventional accounts predict the opposite correlation—noise-floor dynamics suggest small weights freeze late with small final magnitudes, while monotone growth suggests large weights escape noise earlier with large final magnitudes, both yielding a negative correlation between freeze timing and weight magnitude, which contradicts the distinctive conjunction §2 predicts.

I'm specifying the experimental substrate: weight geometry only across EleutherAI's pythia-160m (MLP dense layer, layers 6–11), with 200k fixed-seed coordinates sampled and 20 log-uniformly spaced checkpoints from step 0 through step 143k, each pinned to a specific HF revision.

For each coordinate I'm tracking the sign of the weight matrix, identifying when that sign stabilizes across checkpoints, counting sign flips as a lower bound, measuring final weight magnitude, and tracing the amplitude trajectory. The aliasing discipline treats oscillations as ordinal strata only—no claims about flip rates—and I'm applying gates to validate that all revisions load cleanly, shapes match, step 0 shows symmetric sign distribution, and the final checkpoint matches the published version.

Now I'm checking that at least 1% of coordinates fall into each frozen-by-b5, frozen-in-b11..b18, and unfrozen-at-b19 category, otherwise the analysis is void. For the early-faint test, I'm pooling coordinates frozen by b15 with no flips after step 16k, then running a Spearman correlation between freeze_bin and log final weight magnitude with permutation testing to see if there's a real stratigraphy signal or just noise. The three-band test looks at the bottom-decile weights to check whether freeze_bin shows a bimodal distribution—early-frozen commons versus late/never churn—using a dip test and bootstrap validation.

For the latent-development test, I'm examining early-frozen coordinates (≤b10) to see if the lag between amplitude half-bin and freeze_bin is consistently positive, comparing against a within-coordinate permutation null. The layer-profile test reports the Spearman correlation per layer to show how the stratigraphy signal deepens across layers. Then I'm tallying the verdicts: stratified when both SD1 shows positive correlation and SD2 passes the bimodality test, with a prior probability of 25%.

The other categories are partial-strata (SD1 positive but SD2 fails) at 15%, inverted (SD1 negative, indicating structural damage to the amplitude-learning relationship) at 25%, unstratified or ambiguous (SD1 not significant) at 25%, and void at 10%. The key discipline here is that stratified verdicts confirm the early-faint to late-dense churning pattern in real training, not that the types themselves live in the faint stratum—that's a separate extraction-inversion probe.

For the single model tested, I'm using Pythia-160m since it has the type-register lineage needed; Qwen is out of scope due to lack of public checkpoints. The provenance is stored in results/stratigraphy-dating/pythia-160m/ with metadata pinning revision names, sliced sign and magnitude arrays in npz format, and per-result entries in jsonl. The category distribution sums to 100, which aligns with spectral-bias theory predicting early common learning, though I'm still working through the full implications.

I'm verifying the SD1 pool restriction where frozen bins are constrained to 0-15, excluding churners which are handled separately. The amplitude log handles near-zero final weights with epsilon smoothing. Now I need to write up the findings and decide on the freeze commit workflow—whether to batch the freeze, queue state, and checkpoint together in one commit following the established pattern.

After that comes the harness build and validation before launch—that's more involved, so I should track these as separate tasks: the freeze commit itself, then the harness scripts and exploration work.

Starting with the queue and state edits. For the queue row, I'm swapping the white circle to blue and adding a frozen note—keeping it minimal with "(FROZEN s325 on types-are-a-modulation-scheme §P-STRATIGRAPHY-DATING)". For state.md, I'm inserting a new s325 in-progress block at the top of the session section, right before the sealed s324 block, with the front-selected stratigraphy content.

Now I'm documenting the freeze decision: the checkpoint log schedule is approved, and I'm freezing on the types-are-a-modulation-scheme constraint. The key discriminators are SD1 (sign-discriminating, where mundane physics predicts negative density but the theory predicts positive), SD2 (split-fraction three-band from Michael's dip test swap), and SD3/SD4 as advisory. The a-priori weights are 25/15/25/25/10, and the queue gets marked blue. Next I'll build the harness script to validate planted worlds, then launch it to download, slice, and compute stats without GPU.

After that, I'm committing all three files together as a freeze commit—a memory-only classification with the slug "stratigraphy-dating-frozen", following the pattern of past decision commits. Then comes the big task of building the harness itself.

For the harness script, I need to set up the configuration to use EleutherAI/pythia-160m with a list of revisions spanning step0 through step143000 at 20-step intervals, targeting layers 6-11. The parameter extraction uses the dense_h_to_4h weight pattern, with 200k fixed-seed coordinates. The script downloads each checkpoint via huggingface_hub, extracts the pytorch_model.bin file (~350MB in fp16), and loads just the weight slices I need rather than the full model.

For sampling, the dense_h_to_4h weight matrix is [3072, 768] for the 160m model, and I'm drawing 200k total coordinates across 6 layers proportionally using a fixed random seed over flattened indices. The output persists signs as int8, magnitudes as float16, along with indices and layer IDs into an npz file. Then I compute freeze_bin statistics including SD0-SD4, run permutation and bootstrap tests, and apply isotopic regression to the results.

For validation, I'm checking if sklearn's isotonic regression is available as a dependency; if not, I'll implement the pool-adjacent-violators algorithm inline since it's straightforward. The validation mode generates synthetic sign and magnitude trajectories across stratified, inverted, unstratified, and void worlds, runs the full stats pipeline on each, and asserts the expected verdicts. The output includes a results directory with meta.json containing provenance info like git SHA and seed, plus results in JSON format and the npz arrays, with a final verdict line printed to stdout.

For the checkpoint downloads, I'll manage the huggingface_hub cache by fetching each ~600MB file and deleting it after slicing to keep disk usage manageable across the 20 sequential downloads. Before diving into the harness code, I want to check an existing script like boundary_churn.py to match the repo's conventions for meta.json helpers and overall structure, then map out the tasks: freezing the batch commit with queue state, building the harness with validation support, and launching the run with async monitoring.

I've got the necessary dependencies available, so I can move forward with the implementation. For the harness design, I'm planning to download model checkpoints directly from the Pythia-160m repository using the hub download function, specifying different training steps to compare how the model evolves—the files are in the older PyTorch format and fairly large at around 375MB in fp16 precision.

When loading, I'll use torch.load with CPU mapping and weights-only mode, then extract the specific MLP weight matrices from layers 6 through 11 which have a consistent shape of [3072, 768]. For cleanup, I'll leverage the local_dir parameter to avoid polluting the cache—downloading to a temporary directory and deleting the file after extracting the weights I need.

One thing to watch for: older Pythia repos might only have PyTorch binary format, but I should check for safetensors first as a fallback strategy to handle different checkpoint formats robustly.

For sampling, I'm taking 200k coordinates across the 6 layers—roughly 33k per layer—using a seeded random number generator to ensure reproducibility, then storing the signs as int8 and magnitudes as float16 arrays indexed by the sampled positions.

Now I'm analyzing how signs evolve across the 20 bins: for each coordinate, I track whether the final sign matches earlier bins to determine when it stabilized (freeze_bin), count any sign flips between consecutive bins, and handle exact zeros as mismatches since they break sign constancy. I've loaded all 20 checkpoints and the initial step shows very low mean absolute sign values.

For validation, I'm downloading the main revision to compare against step143000 and verify they match, then defining non-degeneracy thresholds: at least 1% of coordinates should freeze by bin 5, another 1% between bins 11–18, and at least 1% should show their final flip at the transition into bin 19.

For the Spearman correlation analysis, I'm pooling coordinates that froze by bin 15 (no sign flips after step 16k), then computing the rank correlation between freeze_bin and log-magnitude of final weights using permutation testing with 10,000 shuffles to establish significance in both directions.

For SD2, I'm defining deciles of weight magnitude separately within each of the six weight matrices, then pooling coordinates with their decile labels. I'm marking coordinates as "commons" if they froze by bin 10, and "churners" if they flipped at any transition between bins 15–19 (indicating activity after step 16k).

For deciles 2–10, I'm computing the fraction of commons in each, then fitting an isotonic regression to enforce monotone increase. To estimate decile 1, I'm using constant extrapolation (the fitted value at decile 2), which gives an upper bound on the expected trend. I'll flag cases where the observed fraction at decile 1 exceeds this prediction.

To test significance, I'm using bootstrap resampling: resample the per-decile binomial counts (each decile has ~20k observations), recompute the observed fraction and isotonic prediction, and build a distribution of the difference. The p-value is the fraction of bootstrap replicates where the difference is ≤ 0. This is computationally heavy (10k bootstrap × 200k resamples), so I'm optimizing by resampling counts directly rather than individual coordinates.

For the isotonic fit itself, I'm applying PAV (pool-adjacent-violators) over the 9 deciles weighted by their sample sizes. For decile 1, which has no lower decile, I'm extending the fit conservatively by using the fitted value at decile 2 as the prediction—this makes the test harder to pass, which is the right direction of bias.

Now I'm thinking through the SD3 advisory logic: among coordinates where the freeze bin is at most 10 and the bottom-decile weight magnitude is notable, I need to track the amplitude trajectory and find the half-max bin—the first bin where the amplitude reaches 50% of its maximum. The lag is the difference between this half-max bin and the freeze bin, and I'll report the median lag across coordinates. For the null distribution, I'm considering whether to permute the amplitude trajectory or use a circular shift, though the permutation approach would destroy the monotonicity structure I'm trying to preserve.

For SD4, I'm computing the correlation per layer using the same approach as SD1. Then I'll build a verdict tree for each frozen table and validate everything against planted worlds with three distinct populations: commons that freeze early with small final magnitudes, longtail elements that freeze later with large magnitudes, and churn that flips through the bins with small magnitudes. I'm checking whether SD1 shows significant positive correlation and SD2 captures the excess concentration in the bottom decile.

To construct this properly, I need commons and churn concentrated in small magnitudes while longtail occupies large magnitudes, then carefully set the freeze bin boundaries so that commons-defined (freeze ≤ bin 10) captures only the commons population. This way the commons fraction should be high in decile 1 but trend toward zero in deciles 2-10 as longtail dominates, creating a pattern where isotonic fitting on near-zero values produces predictions that diverge sharply from the observed high fraction in decile 1.

Now I'm considering how different scenarios would manifest: if the freeze bins correlate positively with weight magnitude, the SD1 pool captures both early-frozen commons and late-frozen longtail, showing positive correlation; if freeze bins instead correlate negatively with magnitude (the mundane case), that inverts the pattern; if they're independent, the signal disappears; and if everything freezes at the same bin with no unfrozen mass remaining, the whole framework degenerates.

For the validation pipeline, I'm setting up synthetic test arrays that run through the same statistical functions without downloading anything, then checking that verdicts match expectations. The real pipeline downloads data, slices it, saves to npz, computes statistics, and outputs results.json plus a jsonl file with per-gate records and metadata. The metadata captures run identifiers, timestamps, model info, library versions, git commit hash, coordinate counts, layer info, and gate parameters. The results go line-by-line into jsonl format, one entry per gate plus a summary.

I'm following the boundary_churn style conventions with module docstrings, frozen constants, pure gate functions, and argparse setup. For precision, pythia-160m uses fp16 weights but I'll store magnitudes as float32 for safety with logarithms. Memory-wise, loading a full checkpoint is around 650MB transiently, which is manageable. I'll exclude dead coordinates where the scaling factor is zero from all gate calculations but track them in the results. Now I'm writing the main script—keeping it compact but complete, around 450 lines.

I can either import spearman from fuel_theorem for the main result or implement a local rank-based Pearson to keep things self-contained. Now I'm implementing the pool-adjacent-violators algorithm for weighted monotonic regression, which iteratively merges adjacent blocks that violate the increasing constraint. Finishing the merging logic to track how many original elements each block represents, then expanding back to the full output array. For the SD2 analysis, I'm fitting the isotonic regression to the common fractions across deciles 2-10, using the fitted value at decile 2 as the constant prediction for decile 1, and computing the difference between observed and predicted to assess the binomial fit.

For the bootstrap, I'm resampling the counts under the fitted binomial parameters 10,000 times to get the sampling distribution of the delta statistic, then calculating the one-sided p-value as the proportion where delta ≤ 0. For SD1's permutation test, I'm precomputing standardized ranks and doing 10,000 random permutations to build the null distribution of the Pearson correlation, which is computationally efficient even with the large sample size.

Now I'm setting up the bins/steps computation by creating an int8 array of signs across all bins and layers, then working backwards from the final bin to identify where the signs stabilize—computing the freeze point as the first bin where all subsequent bins match the final bin's sign pattern.

I'm also counting sign transitions across bins to identify churners (those with flips in the critical range b15→b19, meaning freeze_bin ≥ 16), commons (stable from b10 onward, freeze_bin ≤ 10), and SD1 pool members (freeze_bin ≤ 15), then organizing the final layer analysis by deciles based on weight magnitudes.

For the actual run validation, I need to check that shapes match expectations, verify the initial sign statistics are near zero, confirm the final values match exactly between the main and step143000 outputs using strict equality, and ensure the non-degenerate fractions meet thresholds.

I'm also planning to add model escalation support with a command-line flag to switch between pythia-160m and pythia-410m, which requires different layer ranges and hidden dimensions for each model. I'll create a model configuration registry that tracks these parameters, then output results to a jsonl file with per-gate statistics.

For the amplitude trajectory analysis on SD3, I need to compute the half-max bin for each coordinate by finding where the magnitude reaches 50% of its peak, then calculate the lag between that point and the freeze bin for common frozen gates in the bottom decile. I'll generate a null distribution by permuting freeze bin labels 1000 times to get a p-value. I'm also validating the gates function with synthetic data across multiple populations and testing edge cases like when there are no churners.

Finally, I'll capture the git commit hash and the uv.lock file hash for reproducibility, and make sure the code stays clean with proper import management.

For downloading models, I'm using huggingface_hub to try loading safetensors first, then falling back to the bin format if that fails—safetensors is already a dependency through transformers. I'll manage the local cache by cleaning up old downloads since the huggingface cache can grow quite large.

mags[:, a] = 0.01 * np.minimum(t / 5.0, 1.0) + 0.001\n        fb_b = rng.integers(11, 15, len(b))  # long-tail: late freeze, dense\n        for fbin in np.unique(fb_b):\n            freeze_at(b[fb_b == fbin], int(fbin))\n        mags[:, b] = 1.0 * np.minimum(t / 15.0, 1.0) + 0.001\n        churn_rows(c, 1)                     # contested: churn, faint\n        mags[:, c] = 0.012 * np.ones_like(t) * (1 + 0.1 * rng.random(len(c)))\n        if kind == \"PARTIAL\":\n            # kill the bottom-decile commons excess: commons get MID mags\n            mags[:, a] = 0.5 * np.minimum(t / 5.0, 1.0) + 0.3\n    elif kind == \"INVERTED\":\n        fb_a = rng.integers(1, 5, len(a))    # early freeze, DENSE\n        for fbin in np.unique(fb_a):\n            freeze_at(a[fb_a == fbin], int(fbin))\n        mags[:, a] = 1.0 * np.minimum(t / 5.0, 1.0) + 0.001\n        fb_b = rng.integers(11, 15, len(b))  # late freeze, faint\n        for fbin in np.unique(fb_b):\n            freeze_at(b[fb_b == fbin], int(fbin))\n        mags[:, b] = 0.01 * np.minimum(t / 15.0, 1.0) + 0.001\n        churn_rows(c, 1)\n        mags[:, c] = 0.012 * np.ones_like(t)\n    elif kind == \"UNSTRATIFIED\":\n        fb = rng.integers(1, 15, n - third)\n        ab = np.concatenate([a, b])\n        for fbin in np.unique(fb):\n            freeze_at(ab[fb == fbin], int(fbin))\n        mags[:, ab] = np.abs(rng.normal(0.5, 0.3, (N_BINS, len(ab)))).astype(np.float32)\n        churn_rows(c, 1)\n        mags[:, c] = 0.012 * np.ones_like(t)\n    elif kind == \"VOID\":\n        mags[:] = np.abs(rng.normal(0.5, 0.3, (N_BINS, n))).astype(np.float32)\n        # everything frozen from b0 => degenerate bins\n    mags[-1] = np.maximum(mags[-1], 1e-6)\n    return signs, mags, layer_ids\n\n\ndef run_gates(signs, mags, layer_ids, rng, *, loads_ok=True, final_ok=True,\n              n_perm=N_PERM, n_boot=N_BOOT):\n    obs = observables(signs)\n    w_final = mags[-1]\n    sd0 = gate_sd0(signs, obs, loads_ok=loads_ok,\n                   final_matches_published=final_ok)\n    sd1 = gate_sd1(obs, w_final, rng, n_perm=n_perm)\n    sd2 = gate_sd2(obs, w_final, layer_ids, rng, n_boot=n_boot)\n    sd3 = gate_sd3(obs, mags, w_final, layer_ids, rng)\n    sd4 = gate_sd4(obs, w_final, layer_ids)\n    return [sd0, sd1, sd2, sd3, sd4], verdict(sd0, sd1, sd2)\n\n\ndef validate() -> bool:\n    expected = {\"STRATIFIED\": \"STRATIFIED\", \"PARTIAL\": \"PARTIAL-STRATA\",\n                \"INVERTED\": \"INVERTED\", \"UNSTRATIFIED\": \"UNSTRATIFIED\",\n                \"VOID\": \"VOID\"}\n    ok_all = True\n    for kind, want in expected.items():\n        rng = np.random.default_rng(99)\n        signs, mags, lids = _mk_world(kind, rng)\n        gates, v = run_gates(signs, mags, lids, rng, n_perm=2000, n_boot=2000)\n        ok = v == want\n        ok_all &= ok\n        sd1 = gates[1]\n        print(f\"  world={kind:<12} verdict={v:<14} want={want:<14} \"\n              f\"rho={sd1.get('rho', float('nan')):+.3f} \"\n              f\"{'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'}\")\n    print(f\"--validate: {'ALL PASS' if ok_all else 'FAIL'}\")\n    return ok_all\n\n\n# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n# Real run: download → slice → persist → gates → verdict\n# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\ndef _download_slices(cfg: ModelCfg, rev: str, idx: dict[int, np.ndarray],\n                     dl_dir: Path) -> dict[int, np.ndarray]:\n    from huggingface_hub import hf_hub_download\n    from huggingface_hub.errors import EntryNotFoundError\n    key = \"gpt_neox.layers.{}.mlp.dense_h_to_4h.weight\"\n    out: dict[int, np.ndarray] = {}\n    try:\n        path = hf_hub_download(cfg.repo, \"model.safetensors\", revision=rev,\n                               local_dir=dl_dir)\n        from safetensors import safe_open\n        with safe_open(path, framework=\"numpy\") as f:\n            for lid in cfg.layers:\n                w = f.get_tensor(key.format(lid))\n                out[lid] = np.asarray(w, np.float32).reshape(-1)[idx[lid]]\n    except EntryNotFoundError:\n        import torch\n        path = hf_hub_download(cfg.repo, \"pytorch_model.bin\", revision=rev,\n                               local_dir=dl_dir)\n        sd = torch.load(path, map_location=\"cpu\", weights_only=True)\n        for lid in cfg.layers:\n            w = sd[key.format(lid)].float().numpy()\n            assert w.shape == (cfg.inter, cfg.hidden), \\\n                f\"{rev} L{lid} shape {w.shape} != {(cfg.inter, cfg.hidden)}\"\n            out[lid] = w.reshape(-1)[idx[lid]]\n        del sd\n    Path(path).unlink(missing_ok=True)\n    return out\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--model\", default=\"pythia-160m\", choices=sorted(MODELS))\n    ap.add_argument(\"--out\", type=Path,\n                    default=Path(\"results/stratigraphy-dating/pythia-160m\"))\n    ap.add_argument(\"--validate\", action=\"store_true\")\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    if args.validate:\n        return 0 if validate() else 1\n\n    cfg = MODELS[args.model]\n    out: Path = args.out\n    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    dl_dir = out / \"_dl\"\n    dl_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(SEED)\n    idx = {lid: np.sort(rng.choice(cfg.inter * cfg.hidden, N_PER_LAYER,\n                                   replace=False)) for lid in cfg.layers}\n    n_total = N_PER_LAYER * len(cfg.layers)\n    layer_ids = np.concatenate([np.full(N_PER_LAYER, lid, np.int16)\n                                for lid in cfg.layers])\n\n    mags = np.zeros((N_BINS, n_total), np.float32)\n    signs = np.zeros((N_BINS, n_total), np.int8)\n    loads_ok = True\n    t0 = time.time()\n    for b, step in enumerate(STEPS):\n        rev = f\"step{step}\"\n        print(f\"[{time.time() - t0:7.1f}s] bin {b:2d} ← {rev}\", flush=True)\n        sl = _download_slices(cfg, rev, idx, dl_dir)\n        flat = np.concatenate([sl[lid] for lid in cfg.layers])\n        mags[b] = np.abs(flat)\n        signs[b] = np.sign(flat).astype(np.int8)\n\n    print(f\"[{time.time() - t0:7.1f}s] published-final check ← main\", flush=True)\n    sl_main = _download_slices(cfg, \"main\", idx, dl_dir)\n    flat_main = np.concatenate([sl_main[lid] for lid in cfg.layers])\n    final_ok = bool(np.array_equal(np.abs(flat_main), mags[-1])\n                    and np.array_equal(np.sign(flat_main).astype(np.int8),\n                                       signs[-1]))\n\n    np.savez_compressed(\n        out / \"strata.npz\", signs=signs, mags=mags, layer_ids=layer_ids,\n        steps=np.array(STEPS), **{f\"idx_L{lid}\": idx[lid] for lid in cfg.layers})\n\n    gates, v = run_gates(signs, mags, layer_ids, rng,\n                         loads_ok=loads_ok, final_ok=final_ok)\n    with (out / \"results.jsonl\").open(\"w\") as f:\n        for g in gates:\n            f.write(json.dumps(g) + \"\\n\")\n        f.write(json.dumps({\"verdict\": v, \"a_priori\": APRIORI}) + \"\\n\")\n\n    def _sh(cmd: list[str]) -> str:\n        try:\n            return subprocess.run(cmd, capture_output=True, text=True,\n                                  check=True).stdout.strip()\n        except Exception:\n            return \"unknown\"\n\n    import huggingface_hub\n    import transformers\n    lock = Path(\"uv.lock\")\n    meta = {\n        \"run_id\": f\"stratigraphy-dating-{args.model}-s325\",\n        \"timestamp\": time.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\", time.gmtime()),\n        \"model\": cfg.repo, \"revisions\": [f\"step{s}\" for s in STEPS] + [\"main\"],\n        \"layers\": list(cfg.layers), \"n_coords\": n_total, \"seed\": SEED,\n        \"probe\": \"§P-STRATIGRAPHY-DATING (FROZEN s325, \"\n                 \"types-are-a-modulation-scheme.md)\",\n        \"git_sha\": _sh([\"git\", \"rev-parse\", \"HEAD\"]),\n        \"lockfile_sha256\": hashlib.sha256(lock.read_bytes()).hexdigest()\n                           if lock.exists() else \"missing\",\n        \"lib_versions\": {\"numpy\": np.__version__,\n                         \"transformers\": transformers.__version__,\n                         \"huggingface_hub\": huggingface_hub.__version__},\n        \"gate_params\": {\"n_perm\": N_PERM, \"n_boot\": N_BOOT, \"alpha\": ALPHA,\n                        \"frozen_by\": FROZEN_BY_B15,\n                        \"commons_max_bin\": COMMONS_MAX_BIN,\n                        \"churn_min_bin\": CHURN_MIN_BIN},\n    }\n    (out / \"meta.json\").write_text(json.dumps(meta, indent=2))\n\n    for g in gates:\n        print(json.dumps(g))\n    print(f\"VERDICT: {v}\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n ### Thinking

I need to sync the code and run validation checks, then clean up a dead loop in the `_mk_world` function's STRATIFIED branch. There's also a logic issue with the churn loop where setting signs at a specific bin position affects the transition behavior and freeze_bin assignment, though the range seems to be cut off mid-thought. Now I'm working through the stratified world case in validate: the commons bin (fb ≤ 10) has a final population magnitude around 0.011, while churn sits at 0.012+, and long-tail dominates at 1.0. When I rank by population within each layer and look at the bottom decile, the commons coordinates—all tied at exactly 0.011—fill the entire bottom decile, giving frac_commons(d1) = 1.0, then deciles 2-3 mix commons and churn before long-tail takes over in deciles 4+.

The isotonic regression on this decreasing sequence pools everything into a flat prediction around 0.25, so my observed value of 1.0 for the bottom decile is way above that, yielding a delta of 0.75 and a bootstrap p-value near zero—the test passes. But I'm noticing the commons magnitudes are all identical at 0.011 exactly, which means rank ties get averaged, and I need to think through how that affects the decile labeling.

With 11.1k commons all having the same magnitude, their average rank lands around the middle of their block, putting them all in decile 2 and leaving decile 1 empty. That's a degenerate stratification that fails the test. I need to add small random jitter to the magnitudes—multiplying by a factor like 1 plus a small random perturbation—so the commons spread across deciles instead of collapsing into one. Now I'm applying isotonic regression across deciles 2-10 with a pattern of zeros and ones, which pools the middling values and predicts the first decile at zero. Since the observed value also equals zero, there's no positive delta, so the SD2 test fails—confirming the PARTIAL-STRATA approach works.

Moving to SD1 in the stratified setting, I'm examining how the commons pool (feedback 1-4 with magnitude ~0.011) and longtail pool (feedback 11-14 with magnitude ~1.0) correlate with log-weight, finding a strongly positive relationship. But when I invert the magnitudes—commons at 1.0 and longtail at 0.01—the correlation flips negative, which is what SD2 expects for the bottom decile under the inverted regime.

For the unstratified case, mixing feedback 1-14 with random independent magnitudes yields near-zero correlation, as expected. The void world introduces random magnitudes across the board.

I'm noticing potential issues with the statistical tests: when feedback is constant, Spearman correlation might return NaN due to zero standard deviation in the pearson calculation. I should add a guard in the gate_sd1 function to catch this—if feedback has zero variance, set correlation to 0 and mark it as non-significant. The z-score calculation handles constant inputs fine by producing all zeros, which correctly flags as non-significant. I also need to verify that the random number generator is being reused properly across the validation runs.

There's some cleanup needed: removing a dead loop in `_mk_world` that iterates without doing anything, and double-checking that the `churn_rows` parameter is actually being used correctly in the stratified SD0 test. The frozen signs initialization looks correct, but I need to trace through the churn logic more carefully—specifically where the final transition forces sign flips at the boundary.

Looking at the real-run validation, I'm checking that coordinates with final sign 0 have `frac_valid > 0.99`, which should be rare with fp32 weights. The bigger question is whether having ≥1% of unfrozen coordinates at bin 19 is plausible given the transition from 100k to 143k steps—that's a 30% increase, so some coordinate flips at the boundary seem reasonable and align with the frozen SD0 spec. There's also a potential issue with the `hf_hub_download` call and local_dir handling in hub 1.x that I should verify.

The safetensors loading with numpy framework is supported, and I can cast the returned arrays to float32. I need to add shape assertions in the safetensors branch since reshape can fail silently. I'm applying several fixes: removing dead loops, adding jitter to magnitudes in worlds, guarding the SD1 constant, adding shape validation for safetensors, and updating the sign assignment logic.

For the freeze_at logic, setting bin0 to a random value that happens to match the safetensors value is fine—freeze_bin will still correctly identify

### Thinking

I'm seeing a significant negative correlation in the unstratified world test with a large sample size making it statistically significant despite the tiny effect size. The puzzle is why there's any negative correlation at all when freeze_bin and magnitude should be independent—I'm tracing through the logic of how the freeze point interacts with bin assignments, wondering if there's some subtle coupling I'm missing in how the random sign and constancy constraints work together.

Now I'm realizing the actual source of the confound: churners that randomly happen to stop flipping early can end up with small freeze_bin values and small magnitudes simultaneously. Since sign flips occur with 50% probability at each bin, a churner needs several consecutive non-flips at the end to freeze early—something like a 6% chance for freeze_bin ≤ 15. These late-freezing churners with small magnitudes create a negative correlation signal that reproduces the noise-floor artifact I saw in the toy model, which is ironic since the unstratified world should be null.

### Thinking

I found that the unstratified toy world accidentally reproduced the noise-floor confound where late-quiescing churners with small magnitudes create tiny negative correlations that appear significant at large sample sizes. I need to fix the world itself by decoupling churner magnitude rather than adjusting the gate.

### Thinking

The test came back inverted as expected with no errors, and the initial pre-registered check failed as the falsifier was triggered. Looking at the data, the first standard deviation shows everything checks out — the step is symmetric and valid with properly distributed bins across frozen and unfrozen categories. The second standard deviation reveals a weak negative correlation with a p-value of zero across the sample.

The commons-fraction increases monotonically with magnitude deciles, rising from 0.133 to 0.555, with the bottom decile actually falling below the trend line rather than exceeding it — exactly matching the mundane prediction. The median lag sits at plus nine bins but shows no statistical significance. Across all six layers, the negative correlation remains consistent and uniform, ranging from −0.076 to −0.099, so it's not isolated to any single layer.

This confirms the inverted result: the early-and-faint conjunction from section two doesn't hold at this scale. The pattern shows that larger weights freeze earlier while smaller weights continue churning later, which is standard physics behavior. The bottom magnitude band is dominated by churners at 73%, and the commons-fraction rises steadily with magnitude, which damages the three-band framework from section four.

The structural damage to section two's amplitude-to-time-to-learn law is real at this register and scale — the frame isn't hiding behind aliasing since the baseline is non-degenerate. The constraints are tight: single small model at 160 million parameters, no register-absent lineage, MLP band only, ordinal dating with 20 bins. The frame needed a pre-registered win but got a pre-registered loss on its first test, so sections two and four must be damaged in-page. Even though this is a negative result, it's still a genuine artifact rather than noise.

Now I'm moving through the task checklist: committing results autonomously, documenting the modulation findings and damage notes under the stratigraphy section, flagging this as a falsifier that fired, and preparing the state update and approval batch for Michael.
