Looking at the statistical findings: SD1 shows a correlation of −0.087 with p approaching 0 across 127k samples, indicating that early-frozen signs are denser while late-frozen ones are fainter—this is just the expected noise-floor churn combined with monotone growth. SD2's commons fraction is still being examined.

The three-band test at §4 has failed—sign-stability among small weights doesn't mark a commons band because small weights are actually 73% churners, with the commons fraction rising monotonically from 0.13 to 0.55 across weight deciles, and the bottom decile falls below even the monotone extrapolation. This also affects the separate three-band-plate check row on Qwen, which uses a different model and register (static sign commitment versus temporal). The temporal test on Pythia damaged the frame, so the Qwen check remains but its motivation is now weakened—I should note this in the queue row and leave it annotated. What survives is the single-model result on the 160m register-absent lineage with the MLP band and ordinal 20-bin dating: the effect size is small at |ρ|~0.09, but the sign is decisive and uniform across layers, directly contradicting §2's law that amplitude should scale with time-to-learn at the weight-sign and magnitude register on real base training.

The weight physics here reads straightforward—large weights commit early, small weights churn late—which undermines the crystal-small-because-learned-fast retrodiction and its claimed mechanism at this register, though the crystal facts themselves remain unchanged as separate measurements. The frame now stands at six retrodictions with one untested novel prediction and two negative causal or preregistered contacts: the flip-conflict failed and the stratigraphy check failed. The differential-photography leg from §2 took a direct preregistered hit, while the §1 modulation leg with LOCKIN remains untested.

The frame isn't dead but §2 and §4 are damaged. I need to be honest about the nuance here: freeze_bin is measured against final sign with ordinal bins, and the amplitude-growth confound is exactly what SD1 was designed to weigh, so the preregistered read holds. The §2 claim could retreat to "function-level commons don't equal per-coordinate commons"—the same kind of retreat as the flip-conflict, where the per-coordinate register may not carry the abstraction—but I have to flag this as a post-hoc retreat, not something licensed by the design. I'm recording this as a possible scope retreat that's post-hoc and unlicensed.

Looking at the data from Pythia-160m across 20 log-uniform checkpoints and 200k dense coordinates in layers 6–11: the correlation between freeze_bin and log final weight magnitude is −0.087 (essentially zero, n=127k, consistent across layers), showing that early-frozen weights are dense while late-frozen ones are faint—exactly what both mundane accounts predict and what §2's early-AND-faint conjunction forbids. Commons-fraction climbs monotonically with magnitude decile from 0.13 to 0.55, but the bottom decile shows 73% churners with commons below the monotone extrapolation (SD2 delta −0.052, p=1.0). The §4 three-band falsifiable also failed on sign-stability.

The modulation-frame from §2/§4 is damaged; the crystal-small-because-learned-fast mechanism doesn't hold at this register, and any shift to function-level commons would be post-hoc. Now I need to check the INDEX.md format and move this row to the complete section as a failed result, then review the queue format to see how to annotate the three-band-plate row with weakened motivation.

### Thinking

I'm working through the closure batch now—adding the result section to the modulation page after the provenance section, incorporating damage notes in two places, and creating a memory file to document that sign-freeze follows magnitude rather than stratigraphy. I need to update the INDEX by appending the result summary to row 50, then manage the queue by moving the completed item from new to complete and annotating the three-band row.

Now I'm rewriting the s325 result block itself. The verdict is inverted based on the pre-registered test firing on the first frame—the falsifier caught that early-frozen weights are dense while late-frozen ones are faint, with a strong negative correlation across all six layers. The split-fraction test failed in the informative direction: the commons-fraction increases monotonically with magnitude decile, but the bottom decile sits below even the monotone extrapolation, showing mostly churners rather than marking a commons band as predicted.

The core finding contradicts §2's conjunction about commons freezing early and ending faint—the actual weight-sign and magnitude register shows mundane physics instead, where large weights commit their signs early and small weights churn late. This breaks the mechanism claimed in §2's retrodiction about crystals being small because they learned fast, and damages the three-strata table and three-band edge predictions.

The extraction-inversion heuristic treating faint+sign-stable as algorithm markers doesn't hold at this grain either—commons-candidates actually concentrate at large weight magnitudes. I'm bounded by a single small model, absent register lineage, MLP band only, and ordinal 20-bin dating, which means any retreat to "function-level commons differ from per-coordinate commons" would repeat the same post-hoc flip already forced on §3, and that move would need to be licensed rather than just available.

The frame's ledger now shows six retrodictions against one untested novel prediction and two pre-registered negatives, with the flip-conflict and stratigraphy tests both failing. What's honest here is that SD2's monotone commons-by-decile curve is itself a clean measurement—the first real commons-census across an actual training run in this project—and it's persisted in the data for verification. I need to add a damage note at the bounds section flagging that the pre-registered stratigraphy-dating test on the three-strata law failed when applied to real base training.

The inversion is stark: the correlation between freeze bin and final weight magnitude is negative and mundane, while the commons fraction actually increases monotonically with magnitude—directly contradicting the early-and-faint conjunction hypothesis at the per-coordinate weight register. The strata table shouldn't be treated as established at any grain. For the three-band section, I'm adding another damage note: the three-band falsifiable test failed in its temporal form, with SD2 showing no excess sign-stable commons among small weights and the bottom decile running at 73% churners, well below what the monotone extrapolation would predict. The negative frame absorbs this structural damage while the static Qwen sign-commitment version stays queued only as a register-present-lineage contrast.

Now I'm documenting this in the memory file—storing the key insight about the inverted correlation and failed stratigraphy test, then updating the queue to move the completed section from new to the failed items list with the full details of why the pre-registered test didn't hold up against the real fossil record. I need to annotate the three-band row with a note that the temporal form failed on Pythia's stratigraphy test, and update the index to reflect that the pre-registered test verdict was inverted—the correlation between freeze bin and final weight magnitude is near zero with the expected sign, and the monotone-increasing fraction pattern holds from 0.13 to 0.55 magnitude.

Now I'm updating the state.md s325 block to a sealed-ready form, replacing the existing content with the full session summary including front selection, scope amendment, freeze, build, run, verdict, and closure ledger, with the next action focusing on the sharpest fronts under §P-TYPE-LOCKIN where the §1 leg remains untested.

## Assistant

## The model

**Pythia-160M** — a small open model from EleutherAI, about 160 million parameters (roughly 1/100th the size of the Qwen3-14B we usually probe). We chose it not for its capability but because of something unique to the Pythia project: **EleutherAI published the model's entire training history** — 154 snapshots of the weights taken during training, from step 0 (random initialization, before it saw any data) all the way to step 143,000 (the finished model). That's the "fossil record": you can watch every weight evolve.

We downloaded 20 of those snapshots (log-spaced, per your amendment: steps 0, 1, 2, 4, ... 512, then 1k, 2k, ... 143k) and looked at 200,000 randomly-sampled weight coordinates from the MLP matrices in the deeper half of the network (layers 6–11 of 12). No text was run through the model at all — we only read the weights themselves at each point in time.

## What we tested

The §2 "differential photography" theory from session 324 makes a specific claim about *how training writes weights*: backprop only records **error** — so a pattern that's common and learned quickly generates little total error, and should end up written **early and faintly**. The theory predicts three kinds of weights:

- **Commons** (shared structure): sign locks in *early*, final magnitude *small*
- **Long-tail** (rare exceptions): accumulates *late*, ends up *large*
- **Contested** (never resolves): sign flips forever, stays near zero

For each of the 200k weights we measured two things: **when its sign stopped flipping** (which snapshot it "froze" at) and **how big it ended up**.

## Why the test was sharp

The boring, no-theory-needed physics predicts the *opposite* pairing: big weights escape the noise floor early (freeze early, end big), small weights jitter around zero forever (freeze late, end small). So:

- **Boring physics:** early-frozen ↔ **large** → correlation negative
- **The theory:** early-frozen ↔ **small** → correlation positive

One correlation, sign pre-registered before we computed anything. No way to reinterpret the result afterward.

## What we found

**The boring physics won.** Correlation came out **negative** (ρ = −0.087, overwhelming statistical certainty at n=127k, same in every layer). Early-freezing weights are the *big* ones. And your split-fraction gate showed the fraction of "early-frozen stable" weights rises smoothly with weight size — the smallest weights are 73% perpetual churners with *no* hidden stable sub-population, which also kills the §4 "three-band plate" idea (that a sign-stable commons band hides among the small weights).

## What it means

- The "commons are written early and faint" law is **contradicted** at the level of individual weights in real training. Weights behave like ordinary noisy physics, not like a photographic plate with strata.
- The attractive story "the crystal is tiny because common structure was learned fast" loses its proposed mechanism — the crystal being 0.1% and ternarizable are still real measurements, just unexplained by this.
- The frame is now 0-for-2 on pre-registered tests (flip-conflict, stratigraphy). Its genuinely novel prediction — lock-time with a capture threshold (§P-TYPE-LOCKIN) — is untested and is now the frame's last strong leg.

Honest caveats: one small model, from the lineage that *lacks* the type register, and we dated sign-freezes at coordinate grain — a "commons" that lives in *combinations* of weights rather than individual ones wouldn't show up here. But that retreat wasn't predicted in advance, so it doesn't rescue anything; it would need its own pre-registered test.

The key insight is that the test statistic must match the null statistic, so if I'm using means for the null, I need to use means for the observed too. I can vectorize this by generating permuted indices directly rather than full shuffles—each iteration just draws a new permutation and computes the mean difference, which is O(n) per iteration. Writing permutation logic... I'm extracting per-coordinate magnitude ratios and reporting their distributions across quartiles. Then I'm setting up validation logic with a verdict dictionary, and building synthetic test worlds with planted effects—early populations frozen at specific bins with constant signs, churners flipping signs at late bins, and magnitudes drawn from matching lognormal distributions so the deciles genuinely overlap. Growth rates vary by scenario: accumulation favors early, uniform spreads equally, erosion favors churners, and void excludes churners entirely.

### Thinking

I've committed the freeze with some incidental working-tree changes to the damage-note page that didn't alter content, and now I'm building the re-read harness.

dict:\n    valid = obs[\"valid\"]\n    n = max(int(valid.sum()), 1)\n    n_early = int((pop == 1).sum())\n    n_churn = int((pop == 2).sum())\n    ok = (float(valid.mean()) > 0.99 and n_early / n >= 0.01\n          and n_churn / n >= 0.01 and len(quals) >= MIN_QUAL_DECILES)\n    return {\"gate\": \"AT0\", \"pass\": bool(ok), \"n_early\": n_early,\n            \"n_churn\": n_churn, \"n_qualifying_deciles\": len(quals),\n            \"qualifying_deciles\": quals}\n\n\ndef gate_at1(g: np.ndarray, pop: np.ndarray, dec: np.ndarray,\n             quals: list[int], rng: np.random.Generator,\n             n_perm: int = N_PERM) -> dict:\n    if not quals:\n        return {\"gate\": \"AT1\", \"delta\": 0.0, \"p_pos\": 1.0, \"p_neg\": 1.0,\n                \"sign\": \"ns\", \"per_decile\": {}}\n    per, wts, med = {}, [], {}\n    pools = []\n    for d in quals:\n        m = dec == d\n        ge, gc = g[m & (pop == 1)], g[m & (pop == 2)]\n        per[str(d)] = float(ge.mean() - gc.mean())\n        med[str(d)] = float(np.median(ge) - np.median(gc))\n        wts.append(len(ge) + len(gc))\n        pools.append((np.concatenate([ge, gc]), len(ge)))\n    w = np.array(wts, float)\n    w /= w.sum()\n    delta = float(np.dot(w, [per[str(d)] for d in quals]))\n    null = np.empty(n_perm)\n    for i in range(n_perm):\n        acc = 0.0\n        for wk, (pool, k) in zip(w, pools, strict=True):\n            p = pool[rng.permutation(len(pool))]\n            acc += wk * (p[:k].mean() - p[k:].mean())\n        null[i] = acc\n    p_pos = float((null >= delta).mean())\n    p_neg = float((null <= delta).mean())\n    sign = \"positive\" if (delta > 0 and p_pos < ALPHA) else \\\n           \"negative\" if (delta < 0 and p_neg < ALPHA) else \"ns\"\n    return {\"gate\": \"AT1\", \"delta\": delta, \"p_pos\": p_pos, \"p_neg\": p_neg,\n            \"sign\": sign, \"per_decile_mean\": per, \"per_decile_median\": med}\n\n\ndef gate_at2(obs: dict, mags: np.ndarray, pop: np.ndarray) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Advisory: fraction of final amplitude present at sign-freeze.\"\"\"\n    sel = np.where(pop == 1)[0]\n    if len(sel) == 0:\n        return {\"gate\": \"AT2\", \"advisory\": True, \"n\": 0}\n    fb = obs[\"freeze_bin\"][sel].astype(int)\n    r = mags[fb, sel] / (mags[N_BINS - 1, sel] + EPS)\n    q = np.percentile(r, [25, 50, 75])\n    return {\"gate\": \"AT2\", \"advisory\": True, \"n\": len(sel),\n            \"R_q25\": float(q[0]), \"R_median\": float(q[1]),\n            \"R_q75\": float(q[2]), \"frac_R_below_half\": float((r < 0.5).mean())}\n\n\ndef verdict(at0: dict, at1: dict) -> str:\n    if not at0[\"pass\"]:\n        return \"VOID\"\n    if at1[\"sign\"] == \"positive\":\n        return \"ACCUMULATION-CONCENTRATION\"\n    if at1[\"sign\"] == \"negative\":\n        return \"EROSION\"\n    return \"UNIFORM-GROWTH\"\n\n\ndef run_gates(signs: np.ndarray, mags: np.ndarray, rng: np.random.Generator,\n              n_perm: int = N_PERM):\n    obs = observables(signs)\n    valid = obs[\"valid\"]\n    fb = obs[\"freeze_bin\"]\n    pop = np.zeros(signs.shape[1], np.int8)      # 0 other, 1 early, 2 churn\n    pop[valid & (fb <= COMMONS_MAX_BIN)] = 1\n    pop[valid & (fb >= CHURN_MIN_BIN)] = 2\n    g = (np.log(mags[WINDOW_END] + EPS) - np.log(mags[WINDOW_START] + EPS))\n    both = pop > 0\n    dec = np.zeros(signs.shape[1], np.int8)\n    dec[both] = _pooled_deciles(mags[WINDOW_START][both])\n    quals = [d for d in range(1, 11)\n             if (both & (dec == d) & (pop == 1)).sum() >= MIN_PER_DECILE\n             and (both & (dec == d) & (pop == 2)).sum() >= MIN_PER_DECILE]\n    at0 = gate_at0(obs, pop, quals)\n    at1 = gate_at1(g, pop, dec, quals, rng, n_perm=n_perm)\n    at2 = gate_at2(obs, mags, pop)\n    return [at0, at1, at2], verdict(at0, at1)\n\n\n# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\n# --validate: planted worlds\n# ══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════\ndef _mk_world(kind: str, rng: np.random.Generator, n: int = 20_000):\n    sf = rng.choice([-1, 1], n).astype(np.int8)\n    signs = np.repeat(sf[None, :], N_BINS, axis=0).astype(np.int8)\n    half = n // 2\n    early = np.arange(half)\n    churn = np.arange(half, n)\n    # early: frozen from b1 (b0 random)\n    signs[0, early] = rng.choice([-1, 1], half).astype(np.int8)\n    # churn: alternate every bin from b1 through b18 => flips after b15\n    for b in range(1, N_BINS - 1):\n        signs[b, churn] = signs[b - 1, churn] * -1\n    # magnitudes: same |W_b11| distribution for BOTH pops (deciles overlap)\n    m11 = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)).astype(np.float32)\n    g = np.zeros(n, np.float32)\n    if kind == \"ACCUM\":\n        g[early], g[churn] = 1.0, 0.0\n    elif kind == \"UNIFORM\":\n        g[:] = 0.5\n    elif kind == \"EROSION\":\n        g[early], g[churn] = 0.0, 1.0\n    elif kind == \"VOID\":\n        signs[:, churn] = np.repeat(sf[None, churn], N_BINS, axis=0)  # no churners\n        g[:] = 0.5\n    mags = np.zeros((N_BINS, n), np.float32)\n    t = np.arange(N_BINS, dtype=np.float32)[:, None]\n    frac = np.clip((t - WINDOW_START) / (WINDOW_END - WINDOW_START), 0, 1)\n    mags[:] = m11[None, :] * np.exp(frac * g[None, :])\n    mags[:WINDOW_START] = 0.5 * m11[None, :]\n    return signs, mags\n\n\ndef validate() -> bool:\n    expected = {\"ACCUM\": \"ACCUMULATION-CONCENTRATION\",\n                \"UNIFORM\": \"UNIFORM-GROWTH\", \"EROSION\": \"EROSION\",\n                \"VOID\": \"VOID\"}\n    ok_all = True\n    for kind, want in expected.items():\n        rng = np.random.default_rng(77)\n        signs, mags = _mk_world(kind, rng)\n        gates, v = run_gates(signs, mags, rng, n_perm=2000)\n        ok = v == want\n        ok_all &= ok\n        at1 = gates[1]\n        print(f\"  world={kind:<8} verdict={v:<26} want={want:<26} \"\n              f\"delta={at1['delta']:+.3f} {'PASS' if ok else 'FAIL'}\")\n    print(f\"--validate: {'ALL PASS' if ok_all else 'FAIL'}\")\n    return ok_all\n\n\ndef main() -> int:\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--strata\", type=Path,\n                    default=Path(\"results/stratigraphy-dating/pythia-160m/strata.npz\"))\n    ap.add_argument(\"--out\", type=Path,\n                    default=Path(\"results/stratigraphy-dating/pythia-160m/amp_trajectory.jsonl\"))\n    ap.add_argument(\"--validate\", action=\"store_true\")\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    if args.validate:\n        return 0 if validate() else 1\n\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(325)\n    z = np.load(args.strata)\n    signs, mags = z[\"signs\"], z[\"mags\"]\n    t0 = time.time()\n    gates, v = run_gates(signs, mags, rng)\n    rec = {\"probe\": \"§P-AMP-TRAJECTORY (FROZEN s325)\",\n           \"strata\": str(args.strata),\n           \"timestamp\": time.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ\", time.gmtime()),\n           \"elapsed_s\": round(time.time() - t0, 1)}\n    with args.out.open(\"w\") as f:\n        f.write(json.dumps(rec) + \"\\n\")\n        for gd in gates:\n            f.write(json.dumps(gd) + \"\\n\")\n        f.write(json.dumps({\"verdict\": v, \"a_priori\": APRIORI}) + \"\\n\")\n    for gd in gates:\n        print(json.dumps(gd))\n    print(f\"VERDICT: {v}\")\n    return 0\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    sys.exit(main())\n"

### Thinking

The verdict is clear: accumulation-concentration beats the uniform baseline significantly. Looking at the decile analysis, all ten deciles show strong overlap support with a massive early-frozen cohort compared to churners. The effect size is striking—early-frozen coordinates grow about 2.65 times more than magnitude-matched churners over the same window, with a log-unit difference of roughly 0.975 and essentially zero p-value.

What's interesting is the heterogeneity in post-freeze behavior: the median coordinate retained about 78% of its final amplitude at freeze, but the distribution is wide—some coordinates were actually larger at freeze and shrank afterward due to weight decay, while others more than doubled. This suggests the early-frozen population splits into different trajectories, but the key insight is that churners must be shrinking or staying flat while early-frozen weights hold or grow, which drives the differential. This is the first pre-registered win for the accumulation view, beating the modal null threshold. The revised framework now has one confirmed positive result while the original modulation sections remain unsupported. I need to fold these findings into the final write-up: update the amplitude trajectory section with the new ledger information, and capture the key insight about early-frozen weights accumulating over time. Now I'm finalizing the results commit with the amplitude trajectory verdict — the early-frozen coordinates show a 2.7× stronger amplitude gain compared to magnitude-matched churners across all deciles, with a highly significant effect (p≈0) and consistent per-decile means. After this, I'll batch commit all the momentum files under pre-authorization and report the findings to Michael. The early-frozen coordinates show differential amplitude accumulation while contested ones cancel out in a magnitude-matched way—this is the first pre-registered positive result for the accumulation-concentration revision. The broader lattice story and type-relevance questions remain unlicensed, and the modulation frame still has untested lock time, but this accumulation finding from the stratigraphy data represents genuine contact with the hypothesis. Marking the queue row complete and updating the SESSION 325 block — I need to revise the header to reflect two closed probes instead of one and add the amplitude trajectory arc, which means rewriting that section with the current text as the base. I need to complete the second edit by updating the ledger section with the actual commit hashes and adding the amp arc paragraph, plus fixing the closure batch reference to reflect the real commits rather than the placeholder text.

Your revision passed its first pre-registered test against a deliberately favored null hypothesis, so I'm committing the results and folding everything into the closure.

## User

explain what we learned?


### Thinking

Michael wants a straightforward summary of what we discovered today. I need to explain the setup clearly — we were testing a theory that said neural network weights behave like photographic negatives, with common patterns written early and faintly while harder concepts get denser, contested information churning throughout. We examined Pythia-160m across 20 training snapshots with 200k weights, tracking when signs stabilized versus final size. The key finding is that the theory's sign prediction was actually backwards — early-freezing weights didn't match what the theory predicted.

Instead, we found something more interesting: weights act like integrators of consistent gradient signal rather than photographic plates. Signs commit first (the direction settles), then amplitude accumulates where the signal stays consistent across training, while contested coordinates cancel out to noise. Michael's revision about lattice accumulation over time fits this picture. When we matched weights by size, early-committed ones gained roughly 2.7 times more amplitude than contested ones as training continued. This flips our extraction heuristic back — the algorithm is likely hiding in the large, early-committed, still-growing weights, which aligns with what magnitude pruning research suggests. The "crystal small because learned fast" mechanism doesn't hold up, so we need a different explanation for ternarizability.

## Assistant

## What we learned today

**One sentence:** weights don't record like photographic film — they *integrate*: a weight's sign is a decision made early and kept, and its magnitude is accumulated evidence, growing wherever the training signal stays consistent and canceling to noise wherever it stays contested.

### The question we walked in with

The §2 theory said training works like differential photography: common structure gets predicted quickly, stops generating error, and so ends up written **early and faintly** — while hard cases accumulate dense late amplitude. If true, the algorithm we want to extract (the lattice, the crystal) would hide in the *faint* weights, and the whole field's habit of studying big weights would be looking at the wrong stratum.

### Finding 1 — that theory is dead (❌ INVERTED)

Reading 20 snapshots of Pythia-160m's actual training history: **early-committing weights are the DENSE ones.** The correlation between freeze-time and final size came out with the exact sign the theory forbade, in every layer. And the small-weight band contains no hidden stable population — it's 73% perpetual churners. The "self-erasure" mechanism (once learned, the write stops) does not describe real training. The extraction-inversion heuristic — "look for the algorithm in the faint weights" — is unsupported. Standard magnitude-pruning lore was right all along about where the function lives.

### Finding 2 — your replacement is alive (✅ ACCUMULATION-CONCENTRATION)

Your revision: the lattice takes *time* — it concentrates by accumulation. The trivial version ("weights grow") is just optimizer physics, so we tested the differential form: comparing weights of the **same size** at step 1k, do sign-committed ones grow more than contested ones by step 143k?

Yes — decisively. **~2.7× more amplitude gained**, uniform across every size band, p≈0. Consistent signal compounds; contested signal cancels. That's concentration, not generic growth — and it beat a null we deliberately weighted *against* the hypothesis.

### The synthesis (the part worth keeping)

Put both findings together and a clean two-register picture emerges:

- **Sign = the decision.** Made early for consistent structure, permanent once made. The 6.9% still flipping at the end are the genuinely unresolved coordinates.
- **Magnitude = accumulated evidence.** Not "how hard this was to learn" (dead theory) but "how consistently training kept voting the same way."

This *re-explains ternarizability without the dead mechanism*: the crystal survives 1-bit quantization not because the lattice is faint, but because **the sign is the durable code** — commitment happens early and holds, while magnitude is just the evidence counter stacked on top. You can throw the counter away; the decision is the information.

### Caveats we're carrying honestly

One small model, from the lineage that lacks the type register; coordinate-grain only; and the 2.7× differential conflates two effects (committed weights growing vs. contested weights canceling) that the next zero-compute read can separate.

### Method lesson

The pre-registration discipline earned its keep twice in one day: it killed a theory we liked *and* certified its replacement — same dataset, same rules, no thumb on either scale. And the fossil-record instrument (public checkpoints, weight-geometry only, no forward passes) turned out to be absurdly cheap: the falsification cost 2.5 minutes of compute and the confirmation cost seconds.
